    {

      "bio": "Her 1986 breakthrough album Control established her as an independent force, while 1989's Rhythm Nation 1814 became the highest-selling album of 1990 and made history with seven top five singles.",
      "bio_500": "She helped define the new jack swing genre with her 1986 breakthrough album Control and made history with Rhythm Nation 1814 as the first album to produce number one hits in three separate calendar years.  Janet was inducted into the Rock and Roll Hall of Fame in 2019 and received the Billboard Icon Award as the first Black woman to receive that honor.",
      "created_at": 1785471978136,
      "current_title": null,
      "data_source_id": 0,
      "deep_bio": [
        "Janet Damita Jo Jackson was born on May 16, 1966, in Gary, Indiana, the youngest of ten children to Joseph Walter \"Joe\" Jackson and Katherine Esther Jackson (née Scruse). Growing up in a working-class African-American family in a two-bedroom house on Jackson Street, Janet was raised as a Jehovah's Witness alongside her siblings. Her mother, a talented musician who played clarinet, cello, and piano, had aspired to be a country-and-western performer and worked part-time at Sears. Her father worked as a crane operator at U.S. Steel and played guitar with a local rhythm and blues band called the Falcons to supplement the family's income. From this modest beginning, Janet would emerge to build one of the most influential careers in entertainment history.",
        "Janet began her professional career as a child actress, starring in the television shows Good Times (1977–1979), Diff'rent Strokes (1980–1984), and Fame (1984–1985). At age seven, she first appeared on stage with her older brothers' group, the Jackson 5. In 1982, she launched her music career with a self-titled debut album on A&M Records. However, it was her 1986 album Control, produced in collaboration with Jimmy Jam and Terry Lewis, that marked her commercial breakthrough and established her as an independent force in popular music. The album's fusion of rhythm and blues, rap vocals, funk, disco, and synthesized percussion helped define the new jack swing genre and made Janet the first female artist to have five top-five hits from one album on the Billboard Hot 100 chart.",
        "In 1989, Janet released Rhythm Nation 1814, a concept album with socially conscious themes that became the highest-selling album of 1990 and won a record fifteen Billboard Awards. The album made history as the first to produce number one hits in three separate calendar years and the only album to achieve seven top five singles on the Hot 100. The iconic \"Rhythm Nation\" music video, famous for its choreography and military-inspired fashion, is considered one of the most influential videos in history. In 1991, she signed with Virgin Records for a reported $32 million, making her the highest-paid recording artist at that time. She further cemented her status with the 1993 album janet. and her starring role in John Singleton's film Poetic Justice alongside Tupac Shakur.",
        "Throughout the 1990s and 2000s, Janet continued to evolve artistically with albums including The Velvet Rope (1997) and All for You (2001), which gave her her tenth US number-one single. In 1995, she collaborated with her brother Michael Jackson on the single \"Scream,\" supported by what became the most expensive music video ever made at that time. In 1996, she signed a new contract with Virgin Records for a reported $80 million. Her work has earned her induction into the Rock and Roll Hall of Fame in 2019, a star on the Hollywood Walk of Fame in 1990, and the Billboard Icon Award, making her the first black woman to receive that honor. Her album Rhythm Nation 1814 was added to the National Recording Registry in 2020 and the Grammy Hall of Fame in 2026.",
        "Janet's professional network extends deeply through her decades-long collaboration with producers Jimmy Jam and Terry Lewis, who helped craft her signature sound across multiple albums. She was introduced at her Rock and Roll Hall of Fame induction by Janelle Monáe, who cited Janet as a primary influence. Her family connections remain central to her story: her brothers Jackie, Tito, Jermaine, Marlon, Michael, and Randy all pursued music careers, with the Jackson 5 becoming one of Motown's most successful acts. Her sisters Rebbie and La Toya also entered entertainment. Janet has credited her father Joe, who managed her early career, with pushing her toward entertainment despite her childhood dream of becoming a lawyer.",
        "Janet's personal life has included marriages to singer James DeBarge (annulled), René Elizondo Jr. (1991–2000), and businessman Wissam Al Mana (2012–2017). She had a son, Eissa, with Al Mana in January 2017. She has also been in a long-term relationship with producer Jermaine Dupri. Throughout her career, Janet has used her platform for philanthropy and activism, receiving GLAAD's Vanguard Award for her support of LGBTQ+ rights and amfAR's Award of Courage for her HIV/AIDS advocacy. She has also been recognized with the APLA Commitment to Life Award for her philanthropic work.",
        "Janet Jackson's influence on music, dance, fashion, and visual storytelling has been profound and enduring. Her work has inspired generations of artists across genres, and her music videos helped transform MTV's programming and demographic reach. With over 160 million records sold worldwide, she ranks among the best-selling music artists of all time. Her career represents a remarkable arc from child star in a famous family to fully realized independent artist who redefined the possibilities for women in popular music."
